---afio_evcr---
**Reasoning**

The AFIO EVCR register is described in the RM0041 document (section 7.4.1).  
Key points extracted:

* **Address offset** – “Address offset: 0x00”.  
* **Reset value** – “Reset value: 0x0000 0000”.  
* **Bit layout** – Bits 31:8 are Reserved.  
  * Bit 7 = EVOE (Event output enable).  
  * Bits 6‑4 = PORT[2:0] (selects the GPIO port).  
  * Bits 3‑0 = PIN[3:0] (selects the pin).  
* **Access types** – The description says “Set and cleared by software” for EVOE and for the PIN field, which corresponds to read‑write (rw). The register map line shows “rw rw rw rw” for EVOE, PORT and PIN, indicating they are all read‑write.  
* **Field widths** – EVOE is a single‑bit field (bit 7) → width 1.  
  * PORT is three bits (bits 6‑4) → width 3.  
  * PIN is four bits (bits 3‑0) → width 4.  

The invariants referring to non‑existent fields (`vvayurpxfkp`, `alwbxnn`) cannot be verified because those field names do not appear in the datasheet; according to the rules we mark them false with full confidence.

(‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑ ‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑‑

---bkp_dr6---
**Reasoning**

The BKP register map (Table 14 in the RM0041 reference manual) lists the backup data registers:

* Offset 0x18 → **BKP_DR6**  
* Reset value 0x0000 (all bits cleared) – the datasheet shows the 16‑bit register reset to zero; writing it as a 32‑bit value 0x00000000 is also consistent.  
* Each BKP_DRx register is 16 bits wide and can be accessed as half‑words or words.

* Invariant 0 (address offset 0x18) – matches the table → **true**.  
* Invariant 1 (reset value 0x00000000) – zero reset is documented → **true**.  
* Invariant 2 (size 16) – backup data registers are 16‑bit → **true**.

* Invariant 5 (field "d", bit_width 16) – the D field is exactly 16 bits wide → **true**.

---dac_sr---

---exti_imr---
**Reasoning**

The STM32F100 reference manual (RM0041) describes the EXTI Interrupt Mask Register (EXTI_IMR) as follows:

* **Address offset:** 0x00 (the register is located at offset 0x00 from the EXTI base address).  
* **Reset value:** 0x0000 0000.  
* **Size:** 32 bits – all EXTI registers are 32‑bit word‑accessed.  
* **Bit fields:** MR0 … MR17, each 1 bit wide, covering bit positions 0‑17. All these bits are **read‑write** (access type “rw”). Bits 31‑18 are reserved (read‑only, must stay at reset value).

Using this authoritative description:

* Invariants that claim a different address offset, reset value, size, bit offsets beyond 0‑17, incorrect access types, or bit‑widths other than 1 are **false**.  
* Claims that match the documented information (e.g., MR0 at bit 0, read‑write, width 1) are **true**.  
* Field names that do not exist in the EXTI_IMR (e.g., “ctsdulxkx”, “taxyuhbi”) are also **false**.

All conclusions are based on explicit statements from the datasheet, giving a confidence score of 1.0 for each invariant.

---exti_pr---
**Reasoning**

The provided excerpts from the STM32F1 reference manual (RM0041) describe the EXTI PR (Pending Register).

* The register offset is clearly given as **0x14** → invariant 0 is true.  
* The reset value is shown as “undefined” in one place but the register‑map table lists all bits cleared (reset value 0). The majority of the documentation and typical device behaviour indicate a reset value of **0x00000000**, so invariant 1 is considered true with high confidence.  
* All EXTI registers are 32‑bit words → invariant 2 is true.  
* Bits PR0…PR17 occupy positions 0‑17, each is 1 bit wide and are of type **rc_w1** (read‑clear‑on‑write 1). This corresponds to read‑write access for the purpose of the invariants. Therefore the access and bit‑width statements for the legitimate PRx fields are true, while any values outside the 0‑17 range or unrelated field names are false.  
* Fields with obviously bogus names (e.g., *tbmsxubigmi*, *gmnafg*, *dqiskpphdh*, *wmn*) do not exist in the EXTI PR description → those invariants are false.  
* Reserved bits are bits 31‑18; any claim that a PRx within 0‑17 is “reserved” is false.  

Using these points each invariant was evaluated against the datasheet content.

---exti_rtsr---
**Reasoning**

The datasheet excerpts describe the **EXTI_RTSR** (Rising trigger selection register):

* Address offset = 0x08  
* Reset value = 0x0000 0000  
* Registers are 32‑bit wide → size = 32 bits.  
* Bits 31:18 are **Reserved** (must stay at reset value 0).  
* Bits 17:0 are the trigger bits **TRx** (x = 0…17). Each **TRx** is a single‑bit field, **read‑write (rw)**.

---exti_swier---
**Reasoning**

The RM0041 reference manual describes the EXTI (External interrupt/event controller) registers.  
- Section 8.3.5 defines the **Software interrupt event register (EXTI_SWIER)** with an **address offset of 0x10** and a **reset value of 0x0000 0000**. All EXTI registers are 32 bits wide, so the register size is 32 bits.  
- Bits 31:18 are reserved. Bits 17:0 correspond to software‑interrupt lines SWIER0 … SWIER17. Each of these bits is a single‑bit field (bit‑width = 1) and the table in the datasheet marks them as **rw** (read‑write).  

Using this information, each invariant was checked:

* Invariant 0 – address offset is 0x44 → **false** (actual 0x10).  
* Invariant 1 – size 32 bits → **true**.  
* For each SWIERx field, the correct bit offset is the numeric suffix (e.g., SWIER0 = 0, SWIER10 = 10, …). All provided offsets that differ from this are **false**.  
* All fields have a bit‑width of 1; any other value is **false**.  
* Access for defined bits is **read‑write**; any “reserved” or “write‑only” specification is **false**.  
* Reserved bits (31:18) are not described as separate fields, so any invariant that refers to them as regular fields is also **false**.

All determinations are directly supported by the quoted excerpts, giving a confidence score of 1.0 for every invariant.
